| D51 | D | **"Ordenar: prazo" is a label, and the screen says in as many words that it cannot be anything else.** Sci, 2026-10-06: *"The sort can be by Value (Asc/Desc); By Time (prazo)."* `radar-view.tsx`'s `FilterRow` carries the sentence this card removes — *"Sorting is by deadline and is not a choice: the list query orders by `proposals_close_at`, and offering a control that changes nothing would be a lie"* — so the work is to make the choice real rather than to draw a control. **Three orders:** `deadline` (today's exact behaviour, and what an absent `?sort=` means), `valueDesc` (*maior valor*) and `valueAsc` (*menor valor*). **The keyset cursor is the work, not the `order by`.** `tenders.ts` states the rule — *a sort key that does not match its cursor silently skips rows at every page boundary* — and `encodeCursor`/`decodeCursor` carry `(halted, proposals_close_at, id)`. Under a value sort the cursor has to carry the value, the sort key and the cursor have to stay one definition, and a cursor minted under one order must never be read under another: it is **tagged**, and a mismatch **restarts the run** rather than skipping rows — a restart can only ever show rows the reader has already seen, while a mismatched comparison hides rows and says nothing. **Nulls last in both directions, which is a measurement and not a preference.** Measured on Neon `main`, 2026-10-06: of **24 162** open tenders **19 574 (81%)** declare an `estimated_value` and **4 588 do not** (567 of them `sigiloso`). Sorted first the 4 588 would own the whole opening page of *menor valor*. **`estimated_value` must sort numerically.** The column is `numeric(16,2)` (verified against production, 2026-10-06), so Postgres already does; the hazard is the **cursor**, where node-postgres hands the value over as text and a missing `::numeric` would put R$ 9 above R$ 10. **`halted` keeps its place** as the first component of every key (`TENDER_STATUS_AND_WATCH` §3.4): a suspended tender sorts below the Divulgada ones under *maior valor* too, and is still never hidden. **The control works before React hydrates**, like the group chips and the filter form: a `<details>` of three real links, because a `<select>` changed with no JavaScript submits nothing. It stays **outside** the filter `<summary>` (WCAG 4.1.2 — "Trocar empresa ou filtros Ordenar: prazo" is not the name of the button that opens the search) and stays absolutely positioned over the row's right end (the form inside that `<details>` is full width). **`pointer-events-none` cannot survive and that is the one property that changes**: a control has to receive the click it is drawn for, so the filter summary keeps its full-width tap target everywhere except under this box. **`listKey` gains the order**, or pressing Back restores a list sorted the other way under the same key — and with it a `nextCursor` cut from the wrong key. **And a sort link must not promote an elected tab into a chosen one** — found by the review of this card's first commit: `query.group` is *the tab on screen*, chosen **or** elected by `bestGroup()`, so spreading it into the three links writes an election into the URL as a decision. The chain closes through `FilterRow`, whose hidden `group` field is gated on `groupChosen`: once promoted, the tab travels with every filter applied afterwards and a later search opens on a tab nobody picked, which may be the empty one `bestGroup()` exists to avoid. The same defect was **already live one route earlier and is fixed here too** — the filter form's `onSubmit` passed `query.group` unconditionally, so the two halves of one form disagreed: with JavaScript the election was pinned, without it the hidden field's gate let it stand. **Copy is a draft for Sci**: `radar.list.sort` is repurposed from *"Ordenar: prazo"* to the label *"Ordenar:"*, plus `radar.list.sortOrders.{deadline,valueDesc,valueAsc}` (*prazo* / *maior valor* / *menor valor*) and `radar.list.sortBy` (*"Ordenar por {ordem}"*, each option's accessible name — the trigger and the active option must not share one name). Nothing here is a new promise, so no `CLAIMS.md` row: the label said *prazo* and the list was ordered by prazo | — | ∥ | 1 | `/radar?…&sort=` and `GET /api/radar/tenders?sort=` take all three orders; **absent is byte-identical to today**, including the cursor strings, asserted against a named `deadline`. A database test **walks every order to its last page** (four pages, three boundaries) and asserts no tender skipped and none repeated — the failure a single-page test cannot see — plus nulls last from both ends, numeric and not lexicographic ordering, `halted` last under *maior valor*, and a cursor from another order restarting rather than skipping. `readSearch` ↔ `radarHref` ↔ `tendersUrl` round-trip including "absent means the deadline order", and `listKey` keys a re-sorted list as a different list. **No address reachable from the sort control or from the filter form carries a `group` the reader did not choose**, and one they did is never second-guessed — asserted as behaviour (every `href` parsed, not a string matched) in `sort-menu.test.tsx` for the links and in `e2e/` for the form's `onSubmit`, which `renderToStaticMarkup` cannot reach; both mutation-checked. The control is three real `href`s in the **server-sent HTML** — asserted in `e2e/` by reading the raw response, not by disabling scripting: `app/radar/page.tsx` wraps the screen in a `<Suspense>` whose fallback is another `RadarView`, and removing a streamed fallback is React's own inline script, so a scriptless browser holds **two** copies of this row. That predates D51 by every parameter and is stated in the spec file rather than worked around. The filter `<summary>` is still not named after the order, and the row is still one line at 390px with the longest label showing — mutation-checked: because that box is `absolute` it does not wrap or push but lands *on top of* the filter label, and making the "Ordenar:" prefix unconditional makes that test fail on exactly that comparison. The prefix is therefore hidden below 480px and the trigger reads just *maior valor* on a phone |
| D52 | D | **The Radar filters by CNPJ, UF and keyword, and by nothing a buyer actually chooses by.** Sci, 2026-10-06: *"The filter can be by: Type: Dispensa, Pregao and the other methods; Exclusive or Not exclusive to ME/EPP"*. Two new controls in `FilterRow`, both reading columns the list already selects. **Built in `task/d52-filters`; every string approved by Sci 2026-10-06.** **One measurement behind every number here: Neon `main`, 2026-10-06 18:19 UTC**, read at one instant because the counts drift with the 30-minute sweep. **(1) Modalidade — filtered on `tenders.modality_id`, labelled with `modality_name`, as a `Select` of three.** Over the 24 763 open tenders: id **6** `Pregão - Eletrônico` **17 259**, id **4** `Concorrência - Eletrônica` **4 016**, id **8** `Dispensa` **3 488** — the same three pairs over all 58 495 rows, **0 null `modality_id`**, exactly one name per id. **The predicate is the code and never the name**, and the first draft got this wrong: `modality_name` is free text PNCP sends, arriving from two different endpoints under two different field names — `modalidadeNome` on the detail API, `modalidade_licitacao_nome` on the search sweep (`worker/licitaqui/tenders.py`) — each stored verbatim. One re-worded hyphen and an equality on that text returns nothing while the Radar tells the reader nothing is open, which is `memory: empty-result-is-not-absence` on the main screen. The name is kept for the option label, where PNCP's words match what the tender screen already prints and being stale is cosmetic. The three codes are `DEFAULT_MODALITIES = (6, 8, 4)` in the worker, the tuple the sweep asks PNCP for; a job payload can override it and `_MODALITY_NAMES` knows ten, so a fourth modality is one payload away — which is survivable **only** because *Todas* adds no predicate at all. A `modality_id in (…the three we know…)` default would hide every edital of the new modality from every reader and nothing would fail. **(2) ME/EPP — `tenders.me_epp_summary`, the same column the card's own tag renders.** Distribution: `none` 15 952, `exclusive` 4 419, `mixed` 1 810, `quota` 496, and **2 086 where PNCP is silent**. **Sci ruled on 2026-10-06 that *Exclusivo* is `exclusive` **and** `mixed`.** A reader choosing *Exclusivo ME/EPP* is asking *where does being an ME/EPP give me a reserved lane*, and a `mixed` edital has exclusive items, so it belongs in that answer. The first version left it out, and the cost was measured rather than argued: **1 810 open editais sat under a heading saying *Não exclusivo* while their own cards said "Exclusivos e cotas ME/EPP"** — the product contradicting itself on one screen, D28's shape with a number on it. What stays in *Não exclusivo* is `quota` (496, tagged *Cotas ME/EPP*), `none` and the silence, and **a cota is not exclusivity** — so nothing under that heading is tagged as exclusive any more. **Both clauses of the negative are `is distinct from`**: `not in ('exclusive','mixed')` is `unknown` for every null and drops the 2 086 silent rows out of *both* buckets — measured both ways, 18 534 against 16 448, exactly 2 086 fewer. "We do not know" is not "exclusive", so silence belongs in *Não exclusivo*, and the two options partition the list exactly: 6 229 + 18 534 = 24 763. **Still open for Sci, found by the review of this card:** `radar.list.allEmpty.body` tells an empty-handed reader to *"Amplie a UF, troque a palavra-chave"* and there are now two more ways to empty all three tabs — *Exclusivo ME/EPP* being the likeliest. **(3) This filter follows PNCP's structured field deliberately, and D36 may move it.** D36 measured that field disagreeing with the AI's reading on **14 of 27** tenders (52%), and the triagem one tap away prints the model's answer; the filter follows the field **because that is what the card's tag already shows**, and it follows whatever D36 rules. Do not re-derive this. **(4) Both conditions go in `scope()`**, the function `listTenders` and `countGroups` share, so *Compatíveis 13* is counted with the same `where`; `listKey` carries both parameters or Back restores a differently-filtered list under the same key; and `radarHref` / `readSearch` / `tendersUrl` / the route's zod object gain `modality` and `meepp`, one spelling on the page and on the request, unlike `uf`/`state`. `listFreshness` is **not** filtered — "atualizado há X" describes the UF's open tenders, as it already did for the keyword — which is deliberate and stated here so the next reader does not take it for a miss. **(5) Five controls and a button need an arrangement that holds at 390px and inside the app shell.** The form measures `min(viewport − rail, 1120) − 2×20` and is **720px** at a 1024px window; one row of four needs ~836px, so the thresholds are container queries (`@container` + `@min-[560px]` / `@min-[880px]`) and not `min-[880px]:`, which would draw four controls in 720px — D29, D30 and D32 for the fourth time. **Handed on, both pre-existing and both named here rather than left in a comment:** `radar.list.filtersHide` (*"Esconder filtros"*) is approved copy rendered nowhere, and `radar.list.filters.label` is the old `radar.list.filters` string (*"Filtros"*) kept under the new namespace — also rendered nowhere, because the `<summary>` deliberately says *Trocar empresa ou filtros*. The `radar.list.changeCompany` shape twice over; they want one line in §5 or deleting, which is Sci's call since the words were approved | D36 | ∥ | 0.5 | Both filters are `Select`s in the one GET form; applying one produces a shareable address with no script (**not** "the Radar works without JavaScript" — the rows come from an effect), and a cold load of that address arrives already filtered, asserted in Playwright on the **first** list request; `countGroups` and `listTenders` are asserted to scope `tenders` identically under every combination, and the tab count is compared to the rows on the page against a real Postgres; the modality predicate is asserted to be `modality_id` and a fixture row carrying the right code under a **re-worded name** is still found by its option; *Exclusivo* returns a `mixed` tender and *Não exclusivo* never does, asserted from both sides on real rows, and reverting `mixed` into the other bucket turns the suite red; *Não exclusivo* returns the rows where `me_epp_summary is null`, and that assertion fails under **both** shorter spellings — `<> 'exclusive'` and `not in ('exclusive','mixed')`; the two options are proven to partition the list, counted rather than reasoned; *Todas* adds no predicate, proven by a fixture with a modality the option list does not know appearing in the list; `readSearch` ↔ `radarHref` ↔ `tendersUrl` ↔ `listKey` round-trip both parameters, and two differently-filtered lists key apart; the five-control row is measured in Playwright at 390px (no horizontal page scroll, nothing past the viewport) and inside the shell at 1024px with the rail out, with it collapsed and at 1440px; no viewport breakpoint above 720px anywhere in `radar-view.tsx` in **either** spelling, including `max-md:`, asserted on the comment-stripped source with the stripper itself under test; and every mutation named above turns the suite red, with a byte-identical restore |

| D54 | D | **`radar.db.test.ts` asserts about rows the suite does not own, so anybody's leftovers can turn a correct test red.** `RUN_ID` scopes what a suite **writes** — and cleans up — but it cannot scope what a suite **reads**, and several assertions in this file read the whole table. The sharpest is *"finds an unmatched tender by keyword, accents and all"*: it picks a distinctive word out of its own tender's `object` and asserts that tender is **on page 1 of 20** of a search filtered by neither agency nor run. Any row anywhere in `tenders` matching the same word can displace it. **Three lanes found this independently on 2026-10-06 and all three were partly right, so all three are here.** **(1) The standing cause, measured by lane 1:** `TEST_DATABASE_URL_R1` holds **162 run-scoped (`99…`) tenders, 58 of them open, with nothing running** — fixtures orphaned by runs killed before their `afterAll`. Those are what displace the expected tender on its page of 20, and lane 1 reproduced the failure **alone, 1 run in 3, with nothing else running**; its own suite writes `TEST_DATABASE_URL_D3`, a different database, so its rows were never visible to that test. **(2) The concurrent trigger, named:** lane 3's filters fixtures (**D52**) wrote `object` values reading `Aquisicao de <token>` — two ordinary Portuguese words — into R1's search index during the parallel window, which is why the same test broke repeatedly that morning. Fixed in `88f4740`. **(3) Why either one is enough:** an assertion over rows the suite did not create has no isolation at all. The same family explains the other victims: *"rejects a CNPJ whose check digits do not add up, before any job"* reads `select count(*) from jobs where kind = 'company_lookup' and created_at > now() - interval '1 minute'` → **0**, so one `company_lookup` enqueued by anybody in that minute reddens a correct validation; *"does not mint a visitor"* counts `visitors` globally around one request; and the `sync_open_tenders` before/after count has the same shape, safe today only because nothing else enqueues that kind during a run. **`--no-file-parallelism` does not help** — sibling files leave their rows behind, so sequencing only changes which assertion notices. **And a green run is not a fix:** with both other lanes finished, `pnpm test` was **1 689 passed, 0 failed** on 2026-10-06 while all 162 orphans were still there — at 1 in 3, one clean run is what this defect looks like most of the time. **The remedy has two halves and only one of them is this card's.** Deleting the orphaned `99…` rows is a **write to a shared database and therefore Sci's** (lane 1 measured them and deliberately left them; the numbers are with him). This card is the other half: make the assertions independent of rows the suite did not create, which also removes the need to keep that database clean for them to pass. **A correction to how D19 first wrote this up, kept because the method is the lesson.** It reported the failures as *"measured pre-existing, not another lane's"*, on the strength of swapping in `origin/main`'s copy of the file and seeing the same test fail. **That control could not exonerate a sibling lane**: it removes D19's own two added cases and nothing else, while lane 3 went on writing into the same database throughout the run. The experiment was sound and the conclusion overshot it — the other explanation was never looked for, which is the shape `memory: check-before-contradicting-a-lane` exists for. **DONE 2026-10-06 in `task/d54-db-test-isolation` — the standing half, and five things the work found.** **(a) What changed, per assertion.** Every list read that names one of this run's own tenders goes through `listOwn`, which sends a run-unique lexeme in `?q=` and `limit=50`. That works because of `scope()` in `tenders.ts`: given a `q` it *replaces* the segment overlap with the tsquery, so the token narrows the universe to this run's 20 rows while `groupExpression` still decides which bucket each one lands in — the grouping under test is untouched, only the table it runs over is. `nextCursor` is asserted null, so *"the tender is in the answer"* is a statement about the whole result set instead of about position 1 of 20. The keyword query is `TOKEN <word>`, and because `websearch_to_tsquery` ands its terms the row still has to match the unaccented word to come back. The `company_lookup` count over a one-minute wall-clock window became a read of the one key the route could have used for that input — `company:<cnpjRef>`, now derived from **this run's own CNPJ** with a check digit shifted, so the key is run-unique — plus `ourJobs()`, this run's two key patterns. The `sync_open_tenders` count became two halves on §4c's line: the mechanism in a direct call to `listFreshness` with a forced-stale `now`, asserting the `Cached.job` it returns is null, and the result through the route (`ourJobs()`, and the key `listFreshness` names, both watermarked by `max(jobs.id)`). The global `visitors` count became four assertions: no `set-cookie`, no row under the request's `ip_hash`, no row under the id a dangling cookie named, and **no CNPJ-less, fingerprint-less row created since a `now()` taken from the database** — asked twice, once with no cookie and once with one, each asserting a 200 first. The tab counts are deliberately left **unscoped**, with the argument written at them: two lower bounds a foreign row can only raise, and one structural zero. Two `not.toContain`s that a page of somebody else's rows would have satisfied carry a positive control each, and the one claim only an *unscoped* list can make — that a segment the company cannot reach is not on the Radar at all — is still asserted unscoped, in the direction where a foreign row cannot lie. **(b) The §4b review of this work is why the row says all that.** Three of the first version's replacements came out **narrower than what they replaced**, every one of them green: the visitor assertions missed the likeliest regression of all — `readViewer` → `readOrCreateViewer` in `app/api/tenders/[id]/route.ts`, one word, which type-checks and inserts a row with a null `ip_hash`, a null `cnpj` and a fresh id, escaping both the fingerprint trace and the cookie trace while the *global* count had caught it; and two assertions keyed on constants (`company:<cnpjRef('11111111111111')>` and `'unused'`) that no cleanup pattern covers, so the first firing would have left a `queued` row, `jobs_dedupe` would have refused every later insert, and the delta would have been satisfied **for ever**. **Scoping an assertion and weakening it are one keystroke apart**, which is the lesson worth keeping. Each fix was mutation-checked as code, with a byte-identical restore: the `readOrCreateViewer` swap reddens exactly *does not mint a visitor* ("expected 1 to be +0"); dropping the check-digit test in `normaliseCnpj`, and separately dropping the route's Zod `.refine`, redden exactly the CNPJ test; `enqueue: false → true` in `listFreshness` reddens exactly the freshness test, naming the `sync_items` job it created. **(c) A measured negative result about the `jobs` table.** A watermark on `jobs.id` over both refreshing kinds was the second attempt at the sweep assertion, and it is **measured not to work**: with a writer enqueueing `sync_open_tenders` beside the suite — which is what the scheduler does on any real database — it failed on that row. A kind and an id say a job is new and never say who made it. **Nothing read from `jobs` can attribute a row to one request unless the key or the payload carries something that request owns**; the attributable signal is the `Cached.job` the enqueueing call returns, which is why the breadth now lives in a direct call and not in a query. **(d) Two defects the file was hiding.** *"finds an unmatched tender by keyword, accents and all"* took the **longest** word of its tender's object — `componentes`, no diacritic — so `unaccent(word) === word` and the whole `pt_unaccent` path was asserted by an ASCII word matching itself; it now takes the longest **accented** word (`informática` → `informatica`), asserts the two differ, and asserts the term survives `websearch_to_tsquery` rather than being dropped as a stopword — which matters more under a conjunction, where a dropped term leaves the token matching alone and the test passing for nothing. And `newVisitor(null)` rows **were never deleted**: `cleanupRun` deletes visitors by `cnpj` and that helper writes none, so every run of this file leaked them. They now carry a per-row mark in `ip_hash` and `afterAll` deletes them, which is one of the ways R1 came to hold **19** visitors nobody owns (measured 2026-10-06). **(e) Cause (2) does not reproduce from today's configuration, and cannot be settled retrospectively.** Resolved 2026-10-06: `filters.db.test.ts` asks for `testDatabaseUrl()` → `licitaqui_test`, and `radar.db.test.ts` asks for `TEST_DATABASE_URL_R1` → `licitaqui_test_r1`. Two different databases, so with the environment as it stands those rows were never visible to this suite — and because the env files are not in git, whether `_R1` was set that morning cannot be checked, which is the finding carded as **D63**. None of this touches causes (1) and (3): an assertion over rows the suite did not create has no isolation whatever the configuration, which is why the remedy is the assertions. Also measured the same day: R1 holds **0** tenders now that the 162 orphans have been deleted, so a green run against it is evidence of nothing — hence the acceptance below. | — | ∥ | 0.3 | **MET 2026-10-06, and not by an empty table.** R1's orphans had already been removed, so 30 runs against it would have proved nothing. The interference was written instead: **80 foreign tenders** under agency `99000000005400`, all open, deadlines 24 h out — ten days earlier than the earliest open fixture (272.5 h, measured), so they take the first slots of every deadline-ordered page — 40 carrying `Informática / TI` (to displace the compatible and check groups) and 40 carrying no segments at all (to displace the keyword group, where a row the company's CNAEs reach cannot appear), every one of them repeating the words of the fixture the keyword test searches for; **plus a concurrent writer** inserting a visitor, a `company_lookup` job and a `sync_open_tenders` job every two seconds for the whole measurement. Against exactly that: **the file as it was fails 7 of its 35 tests** — the keyword test, both compatible reads, the state filter, the CNPJ-validation job count, the sweep count and the visitor count — and **the file as it is passes 30 consecutive runs**, ~100 s each, with the artifact's `md5` checked before every one so the measurement cannot be of a file that changed under it. The interference was then deleted by its own three markers (`agency_cnpj`, a job-key prefix, an `ip_hash`), and **no row this proof did not write was deleted** — the orphan cleanup is Sci's half of the remedy and stays his. The link-to-`/conta/criar` variant was **not** built and is still a card if `events` says most visitors press it. **(3) The copy**: `signedOut` is reused for the 401 and is Sci's; **two new keys are drafts awaiting Sci** — `radar.favourites.failed` (400, 500, a 200 whose body is not `ready`, a dropped connection) and `radar.favourites.tooMany` (the 429), both carrying `_note` lines that say DRAFT. | D57 | D | ~~**Twenty stars on the Radar, all called *Favoritar*, and nothing says which edital.**~~ **Done 2026-10-06 on `task/d56-star-feedback`.** The shape that needed no new string: `aria-labelledby` composes the name from two nodes the screen is already showing — an `sr-only` action word **inside** the button and the **card's own title**. `tenderTitleId` derives the id from the tender id on both sides, so nothing is threaded through as a prop and the reference cannot dangle; the action word is a descendant rather than an `aria-label` so the composed name can never come out empty. **Action first, subject second** — the control still answers to *Favoritar* under voice control, now as a numbered choice instead of a coin toss. `aria-label` is gone from the star. | D58 | D | ~~**The Radar's list snapshot caches a per-identity fact under a key with no identity in it.**~~ **Done 2026-10-07 — one lane with D60, and one discriminator serves both.** `listKey` now opens with `scope`: 22 characters of keyed HMAC over three cookie values — the Auth.js session token, `lq_visitor`, and `lq_scope`, a new `httpOnly` cookie that `POST /api/radar/cnpj` stamps with a keyed digest of the CNPJ it attached to the row. **One value and not two, and the reason is structural**: the snapshot is a single object filled by a single response, so the finest useful granularity is *could this response have differed*, and any mismatch in either half costs exactly one list request because the stars and the grouping come back together. **In the key rather than checked after the hit** (`lib/radar/list-cache.ts`), which is also what closes D55's documented dead end: another caller's list is not rejected, it is **never found**, so there is no path on which `revalidate` merges page 1 by id over it — and two callers' lists coexist, so signing out and back in restores each rather than each overwriting the other. **§12 is met by construction and checked where it lands**: nothing reversible reaches `sessionStorage` — the digest is keyed from `AUTH_SECRET` with its own label, the way `lib/telegram/token.ts` does it, and the session token it covers is `httpOnly` precisely so page scripts cannot have it. **And the journey that reads the jar found that D19's own §12 fix was incomplete.** D19 removed `GroupedBy.cnpj` because it was "the one place an `httpOnly` cookie's CNPJ reached page JavaScript and `sessionStorage`" — and then re-admitted the same value through `GroupedBy.company`, a full `CompanyView`, reasoning from a sentence its own change had just falsified (*"its own `CompanyView.cnpj` is already on the wire because the browser typed it"* — for a cookie-resolved CNPJ the browser typed nothing). `GroupedCompany = Omit<CompanyView, 'cnpj'>` is the completion; nothing in `radar-view.tsx` ever read the field. That cost **one line** in `radar.db.test.ts`, which is D64's lane: its assertion `groupedBy?.company?.cnpj === RUN_COMPANY_CNPJ` **proved the leak** and now asserts its absence. One thing the §12 journey also established, because its first version failed on it: *"the CNPJ appears nowhere in the jar"* is **not a property this product can have** — a MEI's razão social is the person's name followed by the CNPJ, so `legalName` contains it, and the header draws that name. §12 excludes an identifier handed to the client as data, not a substring of a name the reader is being shown. **Tests:** mechanism in `lib/radar/scope.test.ts` (7) and `lib/radar/list-cache-scope.test.ts` (5); the **writer's path** in `lib/radar/scope.db.test.ts` (3), which exists because the journeys never reach a route handler and would stay green if the route stopped stamping the cookie; the **result** in `e2e/journeys/radar-snapshot-identity.spec.ts` (6), which signs out inside the 60-second window, signs in on an account with marks, and reads `sessionStorage` itself. Four mutations, each asserted as code and each restore proved by a byte-identical `md5`: dropping `scope` from `listKey` took down 5 unit tests and 4 journeys and **left green** the two that assert the cache still works for an unchanged caller; the fixture sending a whole `CompanyView` took down the §12 journey; the route keeping the CNPJ took down `radar.db.test.ts`'s cookie-company test; disabling the cookie stamp took down all three db tests. **Left open as D70**: the scope is a bootstrap value and a browser back/forward can leave it behind; **D71** for what the extra keys cost `sessionStorage`. **No new copy — and that is true of `pt-BR.json` and not of the legal documents.** `lq_scope` is a third necessary cookie and §10 of the privacy policy lists two, naming *"um identificador aleatório"*; this one is derived from the CNPJ the visitor searched, lives 30 days, and `/privacidade` is generated from that file at build time. It is a **disclosure** gap and not a §12 breach — `httpOnly`, irreversible without `AUTH_SECRET`, reaching no page script, no `sessionStorage` and no response body. The question is Sci's and is written out in `docs/TO_VALIDATE.md` **#15**, with a row in `docs/CLAIMS.md` for the public exposure; the sentence is deliberately not drafted (legal brief §5). Original card:~~ **The Radar's list snapshot caches a per-identity fact under a key with no identity in it.** `listKey` is `cnpj · state · q · group` (`lib/radar/list-cache.ts`), and since D23's second half the snapshot also carries `favourites` — which belongs to a **viewer**, not to a search. Sign out in the same tab and return to the same search inside `REVALIDATE_AFTER_MS` (60 s) and the restore is `fresh`, so **no request is made at all** and the stars drawn are the previous identity's. Both directions are wrong: after a sign-out, stars for marks that no longer belong to anybody here; after a sign-in, empty stars for an account that has marks on another device — which is precisely the case D23's journey celebrates. `valid()` was carefully taught to reject an entry written before the field existed, a **deploy** boundary; it has no equivalent for the **identity** boundary, which is the sharper of the two because `sessionStorage` survives sign-in and sign-out and does not survive a deploy. **The fix is the same shape as the guard already there** — a discriminator in the snapshot and a reject on mismatch — and the decision is what the discriminator is: not the user id (§12 keeps identifiers out of client storage), so `hasAccount` as a boolean, or a hash, and whichever it is the client needs a signal it can trust. `visitor === null` is **not** that signal: a keyword search never calls `POST /api/radar/cnpj`, so it is null for a visitor too. Narrow window, cheap guard, and the kind of thing that is found by a reader saying *"these are not my favourites"* | D23 | ∥ | 0.5 | Restoring a list snapshot written under a different identity re-reads instead of drawing the previous reader's stars, asserted by a test that signs out and comes back inside the 60-second window; no user id or e-mail reaches `sessionStorage`; and the signal the client decides on is read from somewhere it cannot be wrong about |
| D59 | D | **Two tabs toggling the same favourite both insert, and one of the two taps is lost — and the route's own docstring says otherwise.** `toggleFavourite` is `delete … returning` and then `insert … on conflict do nothing`, and `app/api/tenders/[id]/favorito/route.ts` states that this closes *"exactly the double-tap this control invites on a slow connection"*. It closes the lost-**row** window — the one where a read-then-write lets two taps both see "not marked" and the second insert silently no-ops. It does **not** close the lost-**intent** one: two genuinely concurrent toggles both find nothing to delete, both insert, both answer `true`, and two taps produce one mark. **A toggle endpoint cannot be idempotent** — the request does not say what state it wants — which is why the honest fix is a `PUT { favourite: boolean }` (or `PUT`/`DELETE`) and the client sending the state it is showing. D23's second half closed this within one tab by serialising: `favourite-star.tsx` ignores a press while a `POST` for that tender is open, so no single card can race itself. Two tabs, or a tab and a phone, still can. **Low frequency and it will not be noticed as a bug** — it looks like a tap that did not register — which is the argument for fixing the shape rather than waiting for a report. **Narrow the docstring in the same PR**: a sentence claiming a guarantee the code does not give is the defect `CLAIMS.md` exists to collect, one layer down | D23 | ∥ | 0.5 | Marking is expressed as the state the caller wants rather than as a flip, so replaying a request or racing two of them converges on one answer; a test fires two concurrent requests for one `(user, tender)` and asserts the final row matches the last intent rather than the parity of the count; the opportunity-screen control and the Radar star both send state; and the route docstring says what it now actually guarantees |
| D60 | D | ~~**The Radar caches two lists under keys that cannot see the CNPJ which decided them, and D55 could only switch off the worse one.**~~ **Done 2026-10-07 with D58 — the design is in D58's cell and `lib/radar/scope.ts`; this cell is what it cost and what it bought.** Both shapes are keyed rather than disabled: `wholeListFromCookie` is **gone** from `app/radar/bare-radar.ts` along with its three call sites in `radar-screen.tsx`, and bare `/radar` and `/radar?q=…` are cached like every other search. The scroll restore in `radar-screen.tsx` is the third site D55 left a note on — it was dead for that shape because nothing was ever saved under such a key, and it now moves the window. **What it bought, measured in the browser:** open an edital from a bare `/radar` after three *Ver mais editais*, press *Voltar* — 60 cards, the scroll within 120px, and **zero** list requests. **What it cannot do**, and the assertion that changed to say so: `e2e/journeys/radar-bare.spec.ts`'s fourth test was *"is never served from the cache, because its key cannot see the cookie"*, and it was right — the key was the search alone, so Carla's two clients shared one name and D55's switch-off was the only safe answer. It is now *"is never served another company's list, now that it is cached at all"*: the same journey, asserted as what is on screen rather than as a request count, because the half that was never about caching is the half that still matters. Original card:~~ **The Radar caches two lists under keys that cannot see the CNPJ which decided them, and D55 could only switch off the worse one.** D55 made `/radar` resume the device's last company, and that is the one list `listKey` cannot name: the CNPJ comes from `visitors.cnpj`, which is `httpOnly`, and D19 deliberately kept it out of `GroupedBy` so that no identifier reaches `sessionStorage` (§12). So D55 ships with the snapshot **disabled** for that shape — nothing saved, nothing restored — and the cost is the journey `lib/radar/list-cache.ts` exists for: open an edital from a bare `/radar`, press *Voltar*, and page 1 is re-read, the scroll position is lost and every “Ver mais editais” page is thrown away. That file records what the same shape looked like on production before the cache existed — 60 cards and 3000px of scroll replaced by “Consultando o CNPJ…” — and says it is indistinguishable from having lost the search. **This is D58's shape and should be one lane with it:** a discriminator in the snapshot, rejected on mismatch, exactly as `valid()` already rejects an entry written before D19. D58 needs one for the viewer's identity and this needs one for the grouping, and the deciding question is the same — §12 allows neither a CNPJ nor a user id in client storage, so it has to be something the client cannot be wrong about and cannot read backwards, e.g. an opaque digest the route returns alongside `groupedBy`. **It is two query shapes and not one — found by a review of D55's diff, and D55's first sentences about it were wrong.** `GET /api/radar/tenders` resolves the cookie **before** it checks for a keyword, so `/radar?q=…` with a cookie CNPJ is grouped by that company as well: its `groupedBy`, its `counts` and which tab each row lands in are the cookie's, and only the row set is the keyword's. Scope by phrase, and the ICU `=0` plural branches are part of the sweep's surface. **Done 2026-10-07** — `docs/product.json` gained a `planLimits` block keyed `(plan, feature)` with the `period`, `product.ts` exports it as `PLAN_LIMITS`/`STATED_LIMITS`, `messages.ts` binds `{$triagensVisitante}`, `{$diasVisitante}` and `{$triagensBasico}`, 13 catalogue keys were tokenised — 14 token occurrences, because `radar.landing.faq.columns.0.0.a` carries two — of which two are ICU `=0` branches, `product.test.ts` grew a 12-phrase sweep plus the three terms clauses, and `product.db.test.ts` holds `plan_limits` to the file. The resolved catalogue is **byte-identical** before and after, so no rendered sentence changed. **One measurement in this card was wrong and is corrected:** `3 dias` appears **nine** times, not four — **four** are the visitor window (`radar.landing.plans.basicVisitorGroup`, `radar.landing.faq.columns[0][0].a`, `radar.visitor.expiredTitle`, `radar.errors.visitorExpired`), **five** are the charge reminder (`foundersPage.founderValue.comparisonRows[3].us`, `foundersPage.faq.columns[1][2].a`, `notifications.billingHelp`, `radar.landing.plans.body`, `radar.landing.guarantees[2].body`), and a third three — *"até 3 editais por semana"*, the digest size — is a fourth meaning of the digit. The trap is worse than the card stated, and the phrase scoping is what handles it. **And the acceptance criterion above overstates one thing, so here is what the guard actually does** — found by the §4b review, which is the point of having one. *"fails when **any** catalogue string types one by hand"* is true of `triagens` and not of `dias`. `{$triagensVisitante}` and `{$triagensBasico}` get a real ratchet, a regex built from the row's own value exactly as the plan prices are, because after tokenisation the catalogue holds **zero** literal `N triagens` and the ICU `one` branches say `1 triagem`, singular — so a new string in any phrasing reddens. **One row, two nouns, and the first pass missed the second:** `basico/screening` is sold as *"N triagens … por mês"* **and** as *"ler N editais por mês"*, so a new string using the second noun passed every half — D74(4)'s synonym defect one noun earlier, caught by the review's close-out pass. Both nouns are swept now; the bare `editais` is not and must not be, because the catalogue says `3 editais` of the weekly digest and `8 editais` of the sample copy. `{$diasVisitante}` **cannot have one**: `3 dias` is in five charge-reminder sentences, so the only guard possible is the by-path list, and a brand-new visitor-window sentence saying `3 dias` — or `três dias` anywhere — passes. That is a limit of the fact, not of the effort. The same review also caught two `REQUIRED` phrases that would have gone **false-green**: `até 3 dias` matches §8's CDC withdrawal clause at 7 and the refund guarantee at 30, and `2 triagens por IA` is a strict prefix of Básico's `5 triagens por IA por mês` — both now anchored inside their own §5 row. | D70 | D | **The Radar's cache scope is a bootstrap value, and a browser back/forward can leave it behind.** **Narrowed 2026-10-07 by the review of D58/D60's own diff, which found the card both overstated and understated.** D58/D60 put an opaque `scope` at the front of `listKey` and compute it in `app/radar/page.tsx`, because the restore happens in a `useState` initializer — before the first paint and before any request — so nothing the screen could fetch would be in time to guard it. The prop is therefore only as fresh as the last render of that server component, and **Next reuses a page segment on a browser back/forward without re-rendering it** (its own glossary, verified against `node_modules/next/dist/docs` at 16.3.5: *"Pages are not cached by default but **are reused during browser back/forward navigation**"*, and `staleTimes` *"doesn't change back/forward caching behavior"*). **The reachable path, all in one tab:** bare `/radar` on company A → change company in the filter form, which is `router.push` once React has hydrated → `POST /api/radar/cnpj` stamps `lq_scope` for B → **Back**. The restored page carries A's scope while the route resolves B. **What was overstated:** the original card led on a sign-out, and `signOutEverywhere` is a server action that sets cookies, which **invalidates the client router cache** — so a sign-out *in the same tab* cannot leave a stale scope. Only a sign-out in **another** tab can. **What was understated:** the in-tab company change above, which is the common case, and a second consequence the card did not name at all — with a stale scope a **write** is mis-filed: a full read of B's list is saved under A's key and found by A later, which does not heal. **Rewritten again 2026-10-07, in the same PR, because the first version of this cell said the fix was "deliberately not built" while the branch was building half of it. Half of it is built here; the other half cannot be closed this way.** Past `REVALIDATE_AFTER_MS` the answer arrives and can be compared: `regrouped()` in `lib/radar/list-cache.ts` is asked before `refreshTenders`, and a regrouped answer **replaces** the list instead of merging page 1 by id — which was the D19-shaped outcome (B's header and counts over A's rows, because B's ids match none of A's). `forgetList` drops the mis-named entry and `readAt: 0` stops the save effect writing it back, which is the only reachable part of the write-poisoning. Driven in the browser by `e2e/journeys/radar-snapshot-identity.spec.ts`'s D70 journey — the one test in the suite that presses **Back** — and mutation-checked: comparing the answer with itself leaves the *header* assertions passing and shows **20 of Brilho's cards under Vida's name**, so the card count and a named processo are what do the work. **What is left is the silent window.** Inside `REVALIDATE_AFTER_MS` the restore makes **no request at all**, so there is no answer to compare and no guard can run: the reader sees A's list, coherently, under A's name, while the device is on B. It is bounded by that minute and by the tab's life, and the only real fix is a scope the client can read **at decision time**. Two ways, both with a cost to weigh rather than an obvious answer: (a) the route reports the scope it resolved in `TenderListOk` and the client re-keys from it — but every fixture would then have to produce the digest the server computed for that document, and one that did not would cost every journey a second list request; (b) drop `HttpOnly` from `lq_scope` and read it in the initializer — which removes the lag for the CNPJ half entirely, and **couples the design to `AUTH_SECRET` existing**, because `lib/radar/scope.ts` states plainly that `cnpjTag` digests a dictionary (10^14 before the check digits, and the real list is public) and that what protects it with no secret is that the value never leaves an `httpOnly` cookie. **Also still open, two of them.** A session that stops being valid **server-side** while its cookie stays in the jar flips the route from account to visitor and empties `favourites` without changing any cookie the digest reads — and it need not wait for an expiry: a sign-out **on another device** deletes the `sessions` row immediately. And `readAt: 0` guards **one key, not the document**: it stops the list that proved the scope stale from being written back, and the stale prop then survives for the life of the reused segment, so a later search in that same document is mis-filed again. Narrow, and it is this card's own defect re-entered through the prop rather than a new one. **What B1 taught, and it belongs here because it bounds every option above:** a single digest covering all three cookies was *itself* a worse version of this bug. Our own `POST /api/radar/cnpj` stamps `lq_scope` — and mints `lq_visitor` on a new device — **after** the page rendered, so every snapshot written during the first search of a company was filed under a scope that had already stopped existing, and the next document load missed it: 60 cards and 3 000 px became 20 and zero on the headline *Voltar* journey. Fixed by splitting the scope in two (`RadarScopes`), on the line that the documents whose jar changes under us are exactly the documents whose answer the jar cannot influence. Any fix for the remainder has to keep that line, which rules out "put everything in one digest and read it later" | D58, D60 | ∥ | 0.5 | A snapshot is not restored after a sign-out that this tab reached without re-rendering `/radar`, asserted by a browser test that drives that navigation rather than two `goto`s; a list whose scope the route disagrees with is replaced rather than merged, asserted on screen by a changed company's rows not appearing under the previous company's header; and no journey pays an extra list request for the mechanism | The silent window is closed: a browser Back after the device's company changed, **within** `REVALIDATE_AFTER_MS`, shows the company the route now resolves rather than the previous one — asserted by a browser test that presses Back and makes no request; whichever way it is closed, no journey pays an extra list request for the mechanism, and if `lq_scope` stops being `httpOnly` then `AUTH_SECRET` is required rather than optional, asserted where the key is derived 
| D71 | D | **`sessionStorage` now holds a snapshot per caller as well as per search, and nobody has measured what that costs.** Found in the review of D58/D60, 2026-10-07, and it is a measurement rather than a change. `MAX_SNAPSHOTS = 8` is enforced on the **Map**, and the Map starts empty on every document load — so `evict()` only ever erases a `sessionStorage` entry while one JavaScript context writes more than eight keys. D60 multiplies the number of distinct keys a tab can accumulate by the number of scopes it sees: a device that searches three companies, or a reader who signs in, now leaves entries under keys nothing will look for again, and they live until the tab closes. What to do: measure one entry's serialized length at 20 and at 60 cards, work out how many keys a realistic session accumulates, and only then decide whether `evict()` should sweep storage by prefix rather than follow the Map. So between the two there is a reachable state with neither. It persists: the flag is `localStorage` (`licitaqui.rail.collapsed`), so it survives every navigation and every reload until the reader finds the 56px chevron again, and `localStorage` is also why no media query and no server render can observe it. **This is D20's own acceptance** — *"The strip is reachable from every signed-in route without opening anything at `lg` and up"* — and Sci's instruction for the collapse was *"Collapsed still means present"*, which is what the card's comment in that file says it implemented: *"the column narrows to the toggle and the content beside it widens"*. Note the coupling in both directions: (c) argues *for* keeping those buttons and (a) removes the last argument for them. **No new copy under (a) or (c).** The failing assertion is in place and is `test.fail`-annotated, so Playwright runs it, requires it to fail, and reddens the suite the day it starts passing — and **it is deliberately not scoped to the rail**, because the first version was, and a review found that remedy (b) would have closed this card while that locator still answered zero: a tripwire untrippable by its own fix. The premise below is **not true of `main`**: the suite finishes, and against the 1 175 tests / 2 567 s recorded on 2026-09-30 it has grown **+61% in tests for +6.7% in time**. **`test_integration_billing.py` costs 128.5 s over 15 tests — 4.4% of the branch run, tenth of 28 files** — and cannot produce a run that fails to reach 60% in 7 200 s, because at the measured rate 60% arrives at **1 763 s (29 min)**. **The noise floor settles it**: `test_integration_item_guard.py`, untouched by the branch, drifted **+69.4 s** between the two runs — 54% of the suspect's whole cost — with **111.2 s** of total absolute drift across untouched files, comparable to the entire delta. **Where the time goes is the database's address.** CI runs this same integration suite in **81 s** (job `integration (Postgres)`, run 37944548503, 14:30:27→14:31:48Z) because `ci-worker.yml` stands up a local Postgres and migrates it from `db/migrations`; a local run round-trips every integration test to Neon in `sa-east-1`. A **34× gap from latency alone**, which is why `test_integration_*` carries almost all the time and why editing those files cannot fix it. Not reproducible here — no Docker on this machine, so the 81 s is read off the CI run rather than measured locally. **The one failure is `main`'s** and identical on both sides (`assert 2706.17 < 300`, `assert 2911.52 < 300`): that is **B47**, a drift guard whose threshold is six times shorter than the suite it sits inside, and it is almost certainly the "one failure" of the killed run. The **"one error" is not reproduced** — both runs show one failure and zero errors — so it was contention on the day or a casualty of the kill, and is not attributable from here. **So this card is narrowed, not closed on its suspect**: what remains is to give a local run CI's local Postgres (`db/ci_databases.py` already does it and `docker-compose.yml` is at the root), which would take the suite from ~46 min to ~81 s and make §4b payable again. ~~Original card:~~ **The worker suite stopped finishing, and a suite nobody can finish is a suite nobody runs.** `CLAUDE.md` documents it as **~43 minutes, 1 175 tests, 2 567 s**, measured 2026-09-30. On 2026-10-08 the F2 lane's clean run did **not reach 60% in two hours** and was killed at the harness cap, with one error and one failure already in the log and no summary ever printed — so the failures are unresolved *and* unattributed. **Measure before blaming**, the rule this repo already owes to `coverage_check` and to COST.md: the suspect is `worker/tests/test_integration_billing.py` (478 lines, added on `task/f2-asaas-subscriptions`) against the shared test database, which is where that suite already spends almost all of its time — but the branch carries other new tests too, and that is a hypothesis, not a measurement. This is what §4b costs when it is not payable: *review before merging* depends on the suite returning, and for this branch it does not | — | ∥ | 0.5 | Per-file durations recorded for `main` and for the branch, both in `docs/STATUS.md`, so the delta is attributable rather than argued; the cause named with a number; and if it is the new file, the fix is chosen against that measurement — made to pay for itself, or moved behind a marker — rather than by preference |

---

## 7. After Phase 0 (outline, detailed after Gate 0)

**v1 Essencial · 11-09 → 01-08 (8 weeks, release 2027-01-11)**
- Data: price bands (P25–P75 of winners by state and segment) from `awards`; target purchase price and margin calculator (POC `poc_margem_papel.py`).
- Alerts: `daily_alerts` (10 keywords + compatible CNAE), alert settings screen, filters (ME/EPP, max value, closing date).
- Web: Painel, Edital price/margin, Alertas boards from the canvas; deep analysis UI with quota counter.
- Billing: 3-day charge reminder, plan management, invoices list.
- Parallel lanes: (1) price bands + calculator, (2) alerts, (3) web boards. Holiday buffer: 12-21 → 01-01 reserved for fixes only.

**v2 Pro · 01-11 → 02-26 (7 weeks, release 2027-03-01)**
- Competitors (name, CNPJ, share, discounts), SearchApi market price with normalization, opportunity score, WhatsApp via Evolution API + triggers, OCR for scanned PDFs, new answer keys (construction, continuous services).

---

## 8. Cut lines if M3 (10-08) slips

Cut in this order, without touching the promise:
1. Magic link (keep Google login only) — already the default until a sending domain exists.
2. SSE (keep 3 s polling).
3. "Verificar" group in the Radar (show Compatível + Palavra-chave only).
4. PWA offline shell.
5. Deep analysis before billing (concierge does it manually with the POC script).

**Never cut:** LGPD consent and privacy policy, server-side quota checks, founders seat transaction, webhook idempotency, backups, the "confira no edital" disclaimer on AI output.

---

## 9. Definition of done (every task)

- Acceptance criteria in the task card met and demonstrated on a preview URL (phone for UI tasks).
- Tests for new logic; CI green; no new Sentry errors in preview.
- Copy in `pt-BR.json`, brand spelled "LicitaQui".
- No secrets or personal data in code, logs or fixtures (CPF masked).
- Docs updated when behavior differs from `TECHNICAL_SPEC.md` (and the spec updated in the same PR).
